# Opt-in / skipped-test audit — 2026-11-08

**Two reproducible failures remain.** The full WP22 synthetic dry run fails in both window classes' setup, and the PREQUAL launcher's integration check fails against the current `origin/main`. No product code was changed. One cheap, deliberately failing default-run witness was added. This audit is **not measurement-block clearance**.

## Scope and evidence

| Ref key | Pinned source | Test modules | Reachable skip candidates |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: |
| A | `origin/align/2026-11-08-v4-r11`, `05b6d04cba4868531cc03da187689a3dc187e0eb` | 603 | 664 |
| M | `origin/main`, `8fb75b316b876476cbc55cee46c85d69e2f068c3` | 485 | 526 |
| L | `origin/lane/2026-11-07-b5-cmp-floor-leaves`, `1313698017e8277e4791b368eb8fde0d94748b1e` | 488 | 528 |

The union is **806 test IDs**, with **226 distinct gate expressions/bindings**. These are conditional skip candidates, **not 806 tests proven to skip by default on every machine**. The inventory includes decorators, runtime skips, inherited tests/setup, same-module helper calls, imported test helpers, the G10 function-to-unittest adapter, and expectedFailure on WP22's already-gated collector case. Every candidate has its exact gate, source location, CI classification, observed result or explicit deferral, last established pass, and recommendation below. Explicit `load_tests` implementations were inspected: package hooks canonicalize module names; ALIGN review hooks filter imported duplicate cases, rather than adding an opt-in. Dynamic/metaprogrammed call graphs cannot be proven complete by AST reachability; this is an exhaustive source-construct audit with conservative helper propagation, not a claim to solve arbitrary Python execution.

At intake Git was clean on the supplied `perf/2026-11-08-optin-audit` branch. A filesystem walk confined to `tests/`, pruning restricted directories and not following directory symlinks, found 604 modules after adding the witness; the witness was the only module absent from the tracked baseline. No ignored on-disk test module was found missing from the inventory.

Source audit reproduction (Git objects only; no test import, network, or measurement reads):

```sh
PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1 GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 \
  python3.13 -B docs/perf/2026-11-08-optin-audit-scan.py --output /tmp/optin-gates.json
```

The [machine-readable ledger](2026-11-08-optin-audit-results.json) retains the pinned inventories, per-test observations, complete failure traces, selections, time caps, historical-launcher experiments, and command manifests. PASS applies only to the ref/environment actually executed; it is not transferred across branches because a test file happens to match. Full historical CI execution logs were not fetched (no network); “CI coverage” below means the checked-in workflow actually enables that body, not a verified historical green run.

## Boundary and execution limitations

No analysis pipeline or live measurement campaign was run, no weights were downloaded, and coordination state was not edited. Tests used synthetic fixtures, one unittest module per process, with bounded supervisors. Unittest module processes were serial. One short diagnostic synthetic collection probe overlapped the first corpus batch; that scheduling mistake is recorded rather than claiming all validation work was serial. Capped unittest process wall time was **828.8 seconds**, below the approximately 20-minute test budget. Admission-only probes and the synthetic WP22 diagnosis added only short checks. The existing 8-worker job was not signalled, reconfigured, or inspected.

**Boundary caveat:** initial unguarded dependency discovery with Python 3.14 found optional packages via its home user-site. `find_spec` may have read directory metadata outside the allowed clone; ordinary initial Git commands could also consult global configuration. I cannot certify zero out-of-scope metadata access during intake. No study outcome content was intentionally opened. Subsequent test runs used a filesystem/network fence; later checks also set `PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`. The fence rejected outside-path probes before access. An initial strict-resolution fence also rejected clone-parent probes; it was corrected to resolve within the authorized root without probing those parents. The affected pack test then reached its 30-second cap, so it remains DEFERRED, not a product FAIL.

Python 3.13 had none of NumPy, Jinja2, Torch, transformers, pyarrow, pynvml, jsonschema, matplotlib, or markdown-it available in the allowed environment. The Python 3.14 reruns also skipped optional dependency bodies once outside user-site discovery was fenced. Those are **not optional-path passes**. MLX/Metal was not executed. `nice -n 19` reported `Operation not permitted`; it did not prevent the bounded tests from running and supplied no priority guarantee. No packages were installed.

## CI and default-discovery findings

- **No whole test module is omitted by the main CI shards on any of A/M/L.** `scripts/shard_tests.py` discovers all `test*.py` modules under importable test packages. The only timing-map exclusives are `tests.test_calibration_exits` and `tests.test_calibration_writer_crash_matrix`; each has its own workflow job. Split declarations retain a computed remainder. There is no uncovered exclusive module or missing package initializer.
- The quick tier is narrower: on A, 213 of 603 modules qualify; 390 do not. Its explicit canonical-checkout exclusion set is `test_paper_round7_artifacts`, `test_admit_model_panel_entry`, `test_rpt001_report_slice`, `test_floor_extraction`, and `test_run_campaign`. Those five still appear in the ordinary shards. Unknown timing weights, >=5-second weights, exclusives, and split modules are additional quick-tier exclusions, not omissions from overall CI.
- All main test jobs use Ubuntu / Python 3.13, with 3.11 retained for fences/build/wheel jobs. The source no longer promises a two-interpreter full test matrix. A sets `JW_B5_SYNTHETIC_ONLY=1` in ordinary shards; M/L do not have that added setting in `ci.yml`. The audit set it for every test, independently of workflow drift.
- A's `align-templates` job installs Jinja2 and directly runs `tests.align.test_xml_tool_calls` and `tests.align.test_history_rendering`. Therefore their Jinja2 bodies **do** have dedicated CI coverage, while their MLX/pinned-tokenizer bodies do not. Other optional dependency paths have no corresponding extras job in these workflows.
- No workflow enables `JW_WP22_FULL`, `JOULEWISE_FULL_CORPUS`, `JW_B5_S10_REAL_PROGRAMS`, `JW_B5_FULLSIZE_RECOMPUTE`, the two equivalence acceptance inputs, `JW_ALIGN_NATIVE_TESTS`, `JW_ALIGN_MLX_NATIVE_TESTS`, `JW_ALIGN_REAL_SHADE_TEST`, `JOULEWISE_SHADE_CACHE`, `JW_PREQUAL_HF`, `JW_LIVE_HW`, `JW_REAL_METER_SMOKE`, or `QG_LIVE_DARWIN`. Discovery is not execution of those bodies.
- D117's decisive proof is the exception with a **manual-only** workflow: `.github/workflows/d117-production-proof.yml` sets `JOULEWISE_REQUIRE_D117_FULL_FIXTURE=1` and a registered `JOULEWISE_D117_PROOF_PARTITION`. Its own header says automatic triggering was disabled because of fixture drift. There is no scheduled/nightly workflow on these refs. This is configuration evidence, not a fresh proof that the manual job currently passes.
- There are no pytest slow markers or an independent slow-marker runner in the audited test source. Slow work is hidden by explicit gates or fixture helpers. References to pytest in docstrings do not create pytest-only suites. The old D-101 site-test opt-in is historical: those tests and `site.yml` were removed in the trim; they are absent on all three audited refs and cannot be reported as currently skipped tests.

## Reproduced failures and regression boundaries

| Failure | Observed result | Root-cause commit and evidence | Last established good point | Recommendation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WP22 `CleanWindowTests` (3 methods) and `InjectedWindowTests` (11 methods) | `JW_WP22_FULL=1`: four quick tests run, two `setUpClass` errors; **none of the 14 full-window bodies executes** | `7895b9e7e` makes R11 contrast-fit binding unconditional and initializes a proof with stand-in X count 5. `collect()` later measures synthetic X/Y as 3 turns, changes WP18, and passes the unchanged proof to admission. The checker correctly refuses it. Current admission-only probe FAIL; the identical handoff on parent `199006f16` PASS. No long bisect needed. | `199006f16`: admission only, not a full-window green. Full-run historical record at `d6a9744f0`: 18 tests, one accepted expected failure, 181.390 s (`docs/run_reports/2026-11-06-align-v4-r1-integration.md`, final integration validation). Later full green is not established here. | Keep the new <1 s admission witness in default discovery; run full WP22 in nightly CI and require it in PREQUAL before blocks. |
| New `Wp22AdmissionWitnessTests.test_generated_pilot_turn_counts_refresh_the_contrast_proof` | FAIL, 0.062 s body | Same `7895b9e7e` defect. It mocks only synthetic pilot collection and stops at kit admission, asserting the proof actually matches the newly recorded turn count. It does not weaken kit validation. | No green on current code; regression witness deliberately red. | Default run. Lead repairs the harness in a separate product change. |
| `LauncherDryRun.test_real_launcher_keeps_the_venv` | FAIL at A with M's launcher; two companion launcher tests PASS | First incompatibility: M commit `274e1e7a7` adds default `--keep-going`, but A's PREQUAL checker compares runner argv to the older exact list. Second: M commit `9f56fe78d` requires a 300 s display dwell, so the check's `run --help` deadline of 120 s refuses before touching display state. Pinning the launcher to `7221feca6` (before dwell) retains only the argv failure; pinning to `cad9b67e0` (before keep-going) PASS. This is a mutable cross-ref contract failure, not a claim that M's launcher is intrinsically incorrect. | A's current checker + launcher `cad9b67e0`, executed here. No complete historical A/M integration green inferred. | Default check against an explicit compatible launcher contract; native PREQUAL must also qualify the current launcher. |

The WP22 `expectedFailure` collector-death case is an accepted divergence, not a default skip substitute. On this head its class setup fails first, so even that expected-failure body is untested.

## Executed synthetic opt-ins and incomplete work

Exact entry commands used the selected interpreter and `-B -m unittest` semantics through an isolated result recorder; the ledger preserves every selected test ID and timeout. A path/network fence was active in the recorder. The following are distinct runs, not one full-suite discovery:

| Selection / command tail | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `JW_WP22_FULL=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.align.test_v4_w1_synthetic` | FAIL: two class setup errors, as above. |
| `JOULEWISE_FULL_CORPUS=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.test_scored_packer_stress` | Capped at 120 s; default witness completed, first expanded corpus body unfinished. |
| Same opt-in, `tests.test_scored_packer_fuzz` | Capped at 120 s before completion; no full-corpus pass established. |
| Same opt-in, `tests.test_scored_ownership_forgery` | Capped at 120 s during `test_legal_corpus`; preceding default witnesses completed. |
| Same opt-in, `tests.test_scored_reduce` | Capped at 120 s during the 200-night differential oracle. |
| Python 3.14, `tests.align.test_history_rendering`, `test_xml_tool_calls`, `test_energy_residual`, `test_frontier_inputs`, each separately | Module commands exit 0, but required optional dependency bodies SKIP. Individual skipped bodies remain DEFERRED. |
| Python 3.14, `tests.align.test_energy_residual_regressions` | Capped at 60 s in a default statistical case before the NumPy-specific body. |
| `tests.align.test_r1_round7_close_deferral` | PASS: two parent tests execute the guarded helper in separate controlled child modes; the direct helper entry skips as designed. This is not missing CI coverage. |
| Targeted per-module default guards: flags/catalog/import graph, zstd reader and restore cases, timing declaration, file-system behavior, retained receipt/ref checks | Individual results are in the inventory/ledger. Some zstd/restore and fchflags modules hit 25 s caps; completed individual bodies are recorded as PASS, unfinished bodies remain DEFERRED. |
| M: VDL permission/mode-0 tests and independent producer-pin class | Mode-0 test and all five producer-pin methods PASS; permission module capped at 30 s, with completed methods preserved individually. |
| L: `tests.independent_recompute.test_floor_limit.FloorLimitPinTests.test_words_and_point_floor_rule`; `tests.independent_recompute.test_sol_review_704.SolReview704Tests.test_count_is_a_union_of_normalized_paths` | Both PASS, separate processes, synthetic/code-only. |

The full-corpus opt-in was introduced intentionally by `8a9c03878` (2026-09-29 trim); S10's shared fixture opt-in by `13d9fb671`. Removing intentional expensive coverage from defaults is not itself a defect; having no required alternate execution lane is the uncovered risk.

Forty-five M/L S10 chain/joined tests reach `chain_fixture()` and default-skip unless `JW_B5_S10_REAL_PROGRAMS=1`. Its source documents about 8 GB restoration and hours for a joined driver run; these cannot complete under this audit budget. Full-size recompute uses a 1.2 GB synthetic extraction and serial/3/10-worker comparisons; it was deferred rather than add a 10-worker workload alongside the ongoing analysis. Equivalence acceptance requires full generated S10 result trees, not just a toy equality pair; I did not replace that qualification with weaker tiny fixtures. The archived-corpus, real model-mirror, real meter, clock/SMC, and sealed-journal tests remain unexecuted under their actual prerequisites.

## Required follow-up commands

Run each module separately. These are handoff commands, **not commands to launch while the current job is active**. Tiny native-model checks need the lead's native Mac session outside this no-Metal sandbox; live telemetry/acquisition probes belong to a lead-controlled clean native smoke. Keep measurements and sealed-data replays gated, and retain PROVISIONAL status. All commands use existing offline dependencies/artifacts; no model weights are to be downloaded.

For native tensor/cache/stream tests (synthetic weights/arrays only):

```sh
export PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1 HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1 JW_B5_SYNTHETIC_ONLY=1
python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.align.test_r0_mlx_native
python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.align.test_cache_checkpoint_native
python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.align.test_decode_budget.DeadmanTests.test_mlx_evaluated_token_survives_alarm_during_disarm
JW_ALIGN_MLX_NATIVE_TESTS=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.align.test_thermal_mlx
```

For tokenizer-only native qualification, first provision the pinned non-weight tokenizer files inside the clone; the `.native-tokenizer` below is a required prepared fixture, not something this audit discovered or downloaded:

```sh
JW_ALIGN_NATIVE_TESTS=1 JW_ALIGN_QWEN38_MODEL="$PWD/.native-tokenizer" \
  python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.align.test_history_rendering.NativeHistoryRenderingTests
JW_PREQUAL_HF="$PWD/.native-tokenizer" JW_PREQUAL_OFFLINE=1 \
  python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.align.test_v4_prequal.TokenizerTier
```

For CPU optional dependencies, run each of the following separately in a provisioned offline environment and require that the named optional bodies actually execute: `python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.align.test_moe_hooks`, `tests.align.test_budget`, `tests.align.test_power_nvml`, `tests.align.test_energy_residual`, `tests.align.test_energy_residual_regressions`, `tests.align.test_frontier_inputs`, `tests.align.test_xml_tool_calls`, and `tests.align.test_history_rendering` (expand each module into a separate command). MoE uses tiny random HF configs; missing transformers is not permission to fetch weights.

Live native module commands, after the lead controls the machine state:

```sh
env -u JW_B5_SYNTHETIC_ONLY JW_LIVE_HW=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.align.test_smc_native
env -u JW_B5_SYNTHETIC_ONLY JW_LIVE_HW=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.hazards.test_smc.LiveSmcTests
```

`QG_LIVE_DARWIN` does not itself check the synthetic-only switch; never enable it in a blinded synthetic run. Additional Darwin-only guards (APFS flags/clonefile, kernel boot identity, CommonCrypto, QoS) and Linux-only subreaper guards have their full exact IDs below: use `python3.13 -B -m unittest <that ID>` on the named platform, one module at a time. A Linux-only requirement should be added to Linux default CI, not mislabeled a Mac requirement. External/sealed corpus rows must not be enabled in this clone; replay is a separate authorized post-release task.

On M/L, longer synthetic jobs require a separate budget and prepared synthetic result-tree inputs:

```sh
JW_B5_S10_REAL_PROGRAMS=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.b5_analysis.test_chain_fixture
JW_B5_S10_REAL_PROGRAMS=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.b5_analysis.test_joined_probes
JW_B5_S10_REAL_PROGRAMS=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.b5_analysis.test_dry_run
JW_B5_S10_REAL_PROGRAMS=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.b5_analysis.test_floor_kept_units
JW_B5_S10_REAL_PROGRAMS=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.b5_analysis.test_mint_kept_units
JW_B5_S10_REAL_PROGRAMS=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.b5_analysis.test_release_equality
JW_B5_FULLSIZE_RECOMPUTE=1 python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.independent_recompute.test_member_pool.FullSizeMemberPoolTests
JW_B5_EQ_SYNTHETIC_PAIRS="$PWD/.scratch-s10f/ref::$PWD/.scratch-s10f/cand" \
  python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.b5_analysis.test_result_equivalence.SyntheticTreeAcceptanceTest
JW_B5_EQ_REMINT_SOURCE="$PWD/.scratch-s10f/ref" \
  python3.13 -B -m unittest tests.b5_analysis.test_result_equivalence.S10RemintAcceptanceTest
```

Those example scratch result roots must first be built from the synthetic chain; a missing fixture must fail the required job, not produce a skip-green. `JW_B5_S10_CHAIN_CACHE` must remain under that ref's `.scratch-s10f/tmp` when used. `S10_FULL_CONTROLLER_MEMBERS` and `S10_SPARSE_CALIBRATION` change synthetic fixture depth/cost; `JW_TWIN_FRONTIER_*_WORLDS` changes Monte Carlo budgets; `JW_B5_B3W1_ALL` expands an already-gated external archive replay. These are reduced-coverage modes/expansions, not additional default-skipped method IDs. They must be explicit in the qualifying job's evidence.

The exact next lead action is to repair the WP22 synthetic proof refresh, reconcile PREQUAL with an explicitly pinned launcher contract, run the default witness and full WP22, then add required synthetic/nightly and native smoke receipts with expected test counts and **zero unplanned skips**. Accepted expected failures must be separately named. `unittest OK` alone does not discharge an opt-in gate.


## Per-test inventory

Each row is one test ID; A/M/L cells are independent. `DEFERRED` is deliberately additional to PASS/FAIL/NEEDS-NATIVE: a skipped, timed-out, unprovisioned, or unexecuted body is never a pass and is not necessarily a native requirement. “Unknown” means no applicable successful execution was established, not that the test has never passed. Candidate reachability is conservative; caught SkipTest exceptions can leave a test passing.
